The "Scottisi;(lyclist" referring Co the exhibit at the Stanley Show of the Longford Wire Iron, and Steel Company, Limited, Warrington, Pejo, "This Sr a stall that carries the visitor into the region of the marvellous. Ile will see in one corner 4201b5, dead weight being carried on a single thread of gal- vanised wire ; and in another corner he will find • similar wire supporting RiOlbs. Nor is this all. A woven wire mattress is here bearing a ton weight, without any fear of injurious strain. All this, how-' Byer, is only so much introduction to the chief ex- hibit--the only woven wire saddle in the world, brought to perfection by its inventor, Mr. Woods, only after four years of continuous reconstruction and experiment. It in constructed in three parts, all of which more in harmony with the motion of the legs in pedal ow Felt or glove leather is the . covering, and altogether the seat is an ideal one. AN EXPRESSION OF THANKS.

:Hr.—allow me, through the medium of your valuable : paper, to expresa my sincere thanks for the practical sod heartfelt eympathy which has been - hown unto my • self and family during eur groat trial and bereavement. The lose of a husband is extremely hard to bear, but toe burden has been made much lighter by the great bine, nem shown unto us by all. I should like to rapt.. especial thank. to the railway wrvants who showed their respect for toy decesaed husband by attending the funeral and for their practical sympathy Men to the Whitecross workmen for minder kindnesses. Especial thanks are also due to the members'', the Work ingmen'm Mission Married Men's bible Claes (with whom nip dear husband wan connected) for the trouble taken by them in getting up a concert tar the help of myself and family. My thanke cannot be expressed in worde,nufHee into nay that I. together with threw who have also been wounded by this bereavemf•nt (mother, brothers. k, shall Mateyo feel deeply grateful to all friends for their kindly sympathy.—Tours sincerely, M. EDWARDH Carts. right-street, Warrington, November 25th. 1597.

THE WARRINGTOI TEAM AT ROCHDALE

THIRD aIIC-JESSI-V:li DEFEAT. There were very few supporters of the Warring- ton Club who ventured to follow their team to Rochdale on Saturday, and the faithful few who made thef tourney returned home with blighted hopes. Notwithstandin: the defeats et Runcorn and Salford, it eras felt with some amount of com- lideace that Warrington would, at least, prove more than a match for the Hornets, hut alas for such anticipations, the very reverse wan the case. Start- ing from the Central Station et 12 kit for Manche., tor, and thence by cab in hot haste for the 1.30 train from Victoria Station, the players reached Rochdale—memorable as the birthplace of John Bright -in good time. A pleasant walk throuo the park brought the siti tors near to the football Held in Dissent. A substantial building is erected on the field, and provides ample accommoda- tion for the players—a provision which might with advantage be adopted by other clubs. Although the weather was fine on arrival, there ware evident traces of heavy rains having teee experienced, the field being in • swampy condition. The Warsaw.° team turned out as selected with one exceptio. Hill was too unwell to piley,and his place in the forward. was taken be Jolley. The most noticeable alteration Item the previous week's combination was the substitution of litiesom fur Gross in the three- quarter line,the latter being given a well-earned rest. House was absent from the Rochdale liftmen, his place being taken by W. Rigg,the full back. By a coincidence, therefore, two full backs were play- ing in each team. There would he about 5,031) efictsicwtai.torssiwrheßnrutoctrali.naos were commenced at 3 o h f Stockport, was the re- 'see, and the teams faced each other as Yollows Rochdale L. Itigg,back ; Wightman,avery,Mel- Hew, and W. Rigg,threequarters Ns. and Gar- nett,half backs ; Ashworth,Coupe, fithiron,llarrison, J. tine, Johookine, Stevenson, and Watson, for- wards.
